And it's started costing me real opportunities now, so... I knew I needed to get some help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling overwhelmed or stressed about these forgetful incidents?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I don't know if I'd say I feel overwhelmed, exactly. I mean, it's definitely frustrating, and I know it's a big problem since I'm losing out on jobs and stuff. But I haven't really stopped to think about my stress levels specifically. I don't feel like crying or anything like that, it's just really inconvenient.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I'm definitely annoyed with myself, but I don't feel depressed or down about it. It's just a problem I need to fix, you know? I don't feel like crying or anything like that, it's just really inconvenient.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel anxious or nervous about things. I'm not sitting around worrying all the time. It's really just the forgetting things that's the issue. I just... Like, I'll sit down to update my resume or work on a coding practice problem, and... a text notification will go off, or I'll hear someone talking outside. But whatever it is, it just pulls me completely off track before I even notice what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Even in elementary school, my teachers would always write notes saying I was a bright kid, but... I was constantly losing my worksheets. I would rush through the directions, talk out of turn a lot, and my desk was always just so messy that I could never actually find my assignments when I needed them. So, yeah, it's been happening since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with forgetting things and focusing at work or school, and also when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really affects both. Like, I'll sit down to update my resume or work on a coding practice problem, and... a text notification will go off, or I'll hear someone talking outside. If I have to sit through an interview, or a meeting... or even just a movie, really, I get this... this pressure inside my body. I would rush through the directions, talk out of turn a lot, and my desk was always just so messy that I could never actually find my assignments when I needed them. I would rush through the directions, talk out of turn a lot, and my desk was always just so messy that I could never actually find my assignments when I needed them. So, yeah, it's been happening since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with forgetting things and focusing at work or school, and also when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really affects both. I actually got fired from my last job, so I'm unemployed right now. And even while trying to find a new one... I've completely missed important interview follow-ups. \n\nAnd as for things I enjoy or find interesting... um, well, I was doing this volunteer project that I really cared about, but I was struggling so much to keep track of things that I had to step back from it completely. Honestly, it's just really frustrating because I feel like I can't reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with forgetting things and having trouble focusing consistently throughout your life, or have they varied in severity over time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, I've always had some issues since I was a kid, but the severity has definitely changed. It's been seriously affecting me for about a year and two months now. That's around when things really started falling apart at my biotech job, and it's just continued being this bad ever since I got fired.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty following rules or respecting social norms?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't go out of my way to break rules or, like, do things that are socially inappropriate on purpose. I might accidentally interrupt someone because I get too eager to say something, or I might forget a rule because I wasn't paying close enough attention, but it's never about disrespecting the rules or norms themselves. I definitely try to follow them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um, not really in a big way. I mean, I might buy something small without thinking about it, or like I said, I'll blurt something out in a conversation before I realize I'm interrupting. But I don't do anything dangerous or make huge decisions completely on a whim. It's more just... little things in the moment that I don't catch myself on.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being overly physically active, such as running, climbing, or having trouble playing quietly?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I was overly active like that. I didn't really run around or climb on things all the time, and I didn't have any particular trouble playing quietly. I was more just disorganized and easily distracted, rather than physically hyperactive.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with forgetting things and focusing affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. My friends and family know I can be a bit scatterbrained, but it hasn't caused any real issues or fights. They usually just remind me if I forget something. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focusing and forgetting things in multiple settings, such as at home, at work, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I don't really think so. Like, I know I struggle with focusing and keeping track of things, but I haven't really noticed it being a huge problem across the board. It's like I just need to get up, or shift around, or do something with my hands. If I have to just sit perfectly still, I feel like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these problems with forgetting things and having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it goes way back. So, yeah, it's been happening since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with forgetting things and focusing at work or school, and also when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really affects both. I actually got fired from my last job, so I'm unemployed right now. And even while trying to find a new one... I've completely missed important interview follow-ups. \n\nAnd as for things I enjoy or find interesting... um, well, I was doing this volunteer project that I really cared about, but I was struggling so much to keep track of things that I had to step back from it completely. Honestly, it's just really frustrating because I feel like I can't reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with forgetting things and having trouble focusing consistently throughout your life, or have they varied in severity over time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, I've always had some issues since I was a kid, but the severity has definitely changed. It's been seriously affecting me for about a year and two months now. That's around when things really started falling apart at my biotech job, and it's just continued being this bad ever since I got fired.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty following rules or respecting social norms?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't go out of my way to break rules or, like, do things that are socially inappropriate on purpose. I definitely try to follow them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um, not really in a big way. It's more just... little things in the moment that I don't catch myself on.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being overly physically active, such as running, climbing, or having trouble playing quietly?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I was overly active like that. I didn't really run around or climb on things all the time, and I didn't have any particular trouble playing quietly. I was more just disorganized and easily distracted, rather than physically hyperactive.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with forgetting things and focusing affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. I mostly just know that it keeps happening and I need to figure out how to stop it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down about your situation?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I mean... I'm definitely annoyed with myself, but I don't feel depressed or down about it. It's just a problem I need to fix, you know? I don't feel like crying or anything like that, it's just really inconvenient.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious about anything lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel anxious or nervous about things. I'm not sitting around worrying all the time. It's really just the forgetting things that's the issue. I just... I just want to figure out why my brain keeps dropping the ball on stuff I need to do.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble focusing or paying attention to things?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Like, I'll sit down to update my resume or work on a coding practice problem, and... a text notification will go off, or I'll hear someone talking outside. Sometimes it's even just a random thought about another task popping into my head. But whatever it is, it just pulls me completely off track before I even notice what happened.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. If I have to sit through an interview, or a meeting... or even just a movie, really, I get this... this pressure inside my body. It's like I just need to get up, or shift around, or do something with my hands. If I have to just sit perfectly still, I feel like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these problems with forgetting things and having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Honestly, it goes way back. Even in elementary school, my teachers would always write notes saying I was a bright kid, but... I was constantly losing my worksheets. I would rush through the directions, talk out of turn a lot, and my desk was always just so messy that I could never actually find my assignments when I needed them. So, yeah, it's been happening since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with forgetting things and focusing at work or school, and also when you're doing things you enjoy or find interesting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really affects both. I actually got fired from my last job, so I'm unemployed right now. And even while trying to find a new one... I've completely missed important interview follow-ups. \n\nAnd as for things I enjoy or find interesting... um, well, I was doing this volunteer project that I really cared about, but I was struggling so much to keep track of things that I had to step back from it completely. Honestly, it's just really frustrating because I feel like I can't reliably manage the basic steps of moving my life forward right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these problems with forgetting things and having trouble focusing consistently throughout your life, or have they varied in severity over time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, I've always had some issues since I was a kid, but the severity has definitely changed. It's been seriously affecting me for about a year and two months now. That's around when things really started falling apart at my biotech job, and it's just continued being this bad ever since I got fired.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had difficulty following rules or respecting social norms?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I don't go out of my way to break rules or, like, do things that are socially inappropriate on purpose. I might accidentally interrupt someone because I get too eager to say something, or I might forget a rule because I wasn't paying close enough attention, but it's never about disrespecting the rules or norms themselves. I definitely try to follow them.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um, not really in a big way. I mean, I might buy something small without thinking about it, or like I said, I'll blurt something out in a conversation before I realize I'm interrupting. But I don't do anything dangerous or make huge decisions completely on a whim. It's more just... little things in the moment that I don't catch myself on.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being overly physically active, such as running, climbing, or having trouble playing quietly?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I was overly active like that. I didn't really run around or climb on things all the time, and I didn't have any particular trouble playing quietly. I was more just disorganized and easily distracted, rather than physically hyperactive.\n\nDoctor: Have your difficulties with forgetting things and focusing affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed that. My friends and family know I can be a bit scatterbrained, but it hasn't caused any real issues or fights. They usually just remind me if I forget something. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focusing and forgetting things in multiple settings, such as at home, at work, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um, honestly, I don't really think so. Like, I know I struggle with focusing and keeping track of things, but I haven't really noticed it being a huge problem across the board. Maybe a little bit at home, but I wouldn't say it's happening everywhere I go or in every situation. I'm not entirely sure, to be honest.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a friend or had trouble making friends because of your difficulties with focusing or forgetting things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I've never actually lost a friend over it, and making friends hasn't really been an issue. Like I said, people around me just kind of accept that I forget things sometimes. It hasn't really gotten in the way of my social life.\n\nDoctor: Have you often felt excessively fidgety or restless as an adult?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, when I have to sit through an interview, or a meeting, or even just a movie, I get this... this pressure inside my body. It's like I absolutely need to get up, shift around, or do something with my hands. If I have to just sit perfectly still, I feel like I'm going to crawl right out of my skin.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with focusing, forgetfulness, or restlessness consistently from childhood into adulthood?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's been happening for as long as I can remember. Um, even back in elementary school, my teachers would always write notes saying that I was bright, but I constantly lost my worksheets. They said I rushed through directions and talked out of turn a lot, too. And my desk was always so messy that I could never actually find my assignments when I needed them. So, yeah, it's definitely been a consistent thing since I was little.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble with impulsivity or blurting out answers in multiple settings, like at work, at home, or with friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Um, in regular conversations or even in interviews, I tend to jump in before the other person is actually done talking. I usually do it because I think I know exactly where they are going with their sentence, so I just start answering. But then I realize I completely cut them off, or... or I end up answering the wrong question entirely because I didn't wait to hear the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with focusing or paying attention in multiple settings, such as at home, at work, or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I haven't really noticed having trouble with that. Like, I know I just said I get restless and tend to interrupt people, but as far as actually focusing or paying attention... I don't think I really struggle with that at home or when I'm hanging out with friends. I also had to step back from a volunteer project that I really cared about because I just couldn't keep up with it. So right now, I'm just unemployed and...
